{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014BYN1LCzpTD17KKgZmVuqu","type":"tool_result","content":"   463→              \"filePath\": \"/app/methods.txt\",\n   464→              \"content\": \"Task-switching behavioral paradigm\\n\\nWe designed a behavioral paradigm associated with multiple cognitive processes. In this paradigm, head-fixed mice performed two directional licking tasks that alternated block-wise within each behavioral session. These tasks varied in their cognitive demands but required the same instructed motor output.\\n\\nThis paradigm included an established delayed-response (DR) task in which motor planning is temporally dissociated from movement execution (Fig. 1a). In the DR task, an auditory stimulus indicated the location of a reward. After a brief delay epoch with no auditory stimuli, a separate auditory ‘go cue’ instructed the mouse to move. If the mouse performed a directional tongue movement to the correct target, a water reward was delivered. In separate blocks of trials, mice performed a water-cued (WC) task in which all auditory stimuli were omitted. Instead, a drop of water was presented at a random point in time at a randomly selected reward port. Animals consumed water upon its presentation. Mice received no explicit cues signaling DR and WC blocks. The DR task required a perceptual decision that guides the planning of a subsequent motor action. In the WC task, uncertainty in the timing and location of reward prevented formation of a motor plan before reward presentation, and, instead, mice detected reward availability using olfactory and/or vibrissal cues. This structure required animals to maintain an internal representation of the current block identity (context) to maintain high task performance.\\n\\nWe examined uninstructed movements in this behavioral paradigm using high-speed video. We tracked movement of the tongue, jaw, nose and paws and calculated instantaneous motion energy to quantify movement in a feature-agnostic manner (Fig. 1d–f). We found that mice performed uninstructed movements that varied in their identity and timing across both trials and contexts (Fig. 1e and Supplementary Movie 1).\\n\\nTo assess cortical involvement in instructed movements (directional licking) in both behavioral tasks, we used optogenetic photoinactivation to inhibit the ALM and tjM1 at the go cue (DR) or water presentation (WC) bilaterally in VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice (10 sessions, four mice). Simultaneous photoinactivation of both regions at the go cue (DR task) or water drop (WC task) impaired the initiation of movement in both contexts (Fig. 1b,c; all DR trials: 41 ± 20% reduction, mean ± s.d., P = 1 × 10−4; all WC trials: 15 ± 20%, P = 0.04; and Extended Data Fig. 1c,d). To assess cortical involvement in the expression of uninstructed movements, and whether these movements might be controlled by ALM or tjM1 preferentially, we bilaterally silenced the ALM or tjM1 individually during the delay epoch of DR trials (ALM: 15 sessions, tjM1: nine sessions, four mice). Uninstructed movements were suppressed by photoinactivation of either area (Extended Data Fig. 1b; ALM: 53 ± 31% reduction, mean ± s.d., P = 3 × 10−4; tjM1: 62 ± 24% reduction, P = 2 × 10−3, paired t-test) and more strongly suppressed by concurrent photoinactivation of both areas (Fig. 1g,h; 29 sessions, four mice; 73 ± 35% reduction, mean ± s.d., P = 7 × 10−9, paired t-test), similar to previous observations21 (but see ref. 34). In the DR task, delay photoinactivation of both areas led to a significant decrease in overall performance (Fig. 1i; 22% ± 14% reduction, mean ± s.d., P = 2 × 10−6 paired t-test; Extended Data Fig. 1b) as expected from the disruption of choice/planning dynamics, often leading to animals responding consistently to a preferred side rather than in a manner commensurate with the sensory cue. These results indicate that both the ALM and tjM1 are involved in the generation of uninstructed movements and the planning and execution of instructed directional tongue movements in the two-context paradigm.\\n\\nNeural dynamics related to task variables\\n\\nWhile movement-related and task-related information is encoded in both the ALM and tjM1 in the DR task and other directional licking tasks33, task variables are more strongly represented in the ALM33,36, and so we focused our subsequent analyses there. We recorded activity extracellularly in the ALM with high-density silicon probes during the two-context paradigm to track dynamics associated with planning, context and the execution of movements (Fig. 2; two-context paradigm: 12 sessions, six mice, 522 units, including 214 well-isolated single units; an additional three mice were trained only on the DR task: 25 sessions, nine mice, 1,651 units, including 483 well-isolated single units; see Extended Data Fig. 2a for per-session statistics). In the DR task, we found that choice selectivity (right versus left DR trials) was widespread across all task epochs as expected (sample: 36%; delay: 42%; response: 58% of 483 single units; 25 sessions, nine mice). Individual units showed a variety of activity patterns across trial types, including delay epoch preparatory activity, which has often been taken to be a neural signature of motor planning32,37 (Fig. 2b, left). Individual units were also selective for behavioral context during all task epochs, including during the inter-trial interval (ITI) (39% of single units, 12 sessions, six mice, 214 single units), suggesting persistent coding of context (Fig. 2b, right).\\n\\nMethods\\n\\nMouse behavior\\n\\nBehavioral training was conducted using custom scripts written for Bpod r0.5 (https://www.sanworks.io/shop/products.php?productFamily=bpod). All mice used in experiments were first trained on the DR task with a fixed delay epoch until they reached at least 70% accuracy. At the beginning of each trial, one of two auditory tones lasting 1.3 s was played; the tone indicating a ‘right’ trial was white noise, and the tone indicating a ‘left’ trial was a pure tone (8-kHz pulses). The delay epoch (0.9 s for 12 mice, 0.7 s for one mouse and linearly time warped to 0.9 s) started after the completion of the sample tone. After the delay epoch, an auditory go cue was played (swept-frequency cosine (chirp), 10 ms), after which animals were rewarded with approximately 3 µl of water for contacting the correct lickport. Lickport contacts before the response epoch resulted in the current task epoch restarting, ensuring that they could not progress to the next task epoch until they had stopped licking for the specified length of the current epoch. If the animal did not respond within 3 s of the go cue, this was considered an ‘ignore’ trial, although responses were typically registered within 300 ms of the go cue.\\n\\nMice used for the two-context task (Figs. 1, 2, 4 and 8 and Extended Data Figs. 1–3 and 9) were introduced to the WC context after they were fully trained on the DR context and at least 2 d before the first electrophysiology recording session. A behavioral session began with approximately 100 DR trials and was then followed by alternating blocks of WC and DR trials. Each interleaved block was 10–25 trials, with no fixed block duration. All sessions started with DR trials. Pilot sessions beginning with WC trials had to be terminated early due to a high ‘ignore’ rate, likely due to the mouse becoming sated early in the session. In a WC trial, all auditory cues were omitted, and an approximately 3-µl water reward was presented randomly from either lickport. Trials in which the animal contacted the lickport before the reward (‘early lick’) were omitted from analyses. ITIs were randomly drawn from an exponential distribution with mean 1.5 s. There were no explicit cues during the ITI to indicate to the animal which context block it was in.\\n\\nFor the randomized delay task (Fig. 3g,h,i), mice were first fully trained on the fixed delay DR task (delay duration of 0.9 s). The delay epoch length was then randomized; the duration for each trial was randomly selected from six possible values (0.3 s, 0.6 s, 1.2 s, 1.8 s, 2.4 s and 3.6 s). The probability of each delay duration being selected was assigned such that it resembled a probability density function of an exponential distribution with τ = 0.9 s, as in ref. 44. Animals were trained on this version of the task until they became experts (>70% accuracy and <20% early lick rate).\\n\\nVideography analysis\\n\\nHigh-speed video was captured (400-Hz frame rate) from two cameras (FLIR, Blackfly monochrome camera, BFS-U3-16S2M-CS). One provided a side view of the mouse, and the other provided a bottom view. We tracked the movements of specific body features using DeepLabCut (Figs. 1, 3 and 4). The tongue, jaw and nose were tracked using both cameras, whereas the paws were tracked using only the bottom view. Position and velocity of each tracked feature was calculated from each camera. The x and y position of each kinematic feature was extracted from the output of DeepLabCut. Missing values were filled in with the nearest available value for all features, except for the tongue. The velocity of each feature was then calculated as the first-order derivative of the position vector. Tongue angle and length were found using the bottom camera. Tongue angle was defined as the angle between the vector pointing from the jaw to the tip of the tongue and the vector defining the direction the mouse was facing. Tongue length was calculated as the Euclidean distance from the jaw to the tip of the tongue.\\n\\nMotion energy\\n\\nThe motion energy for a given frame and pixel was defined as the absolute value of the difference between the median value of the pixel across the next five frames (12.5 ms) and the median value of the pixel across the previous five frames (12.5 ms). Motion energy for each frame was then converted to a single value by taking the 99th percentile (~700 pixels) of motion energy values across the frame. Motion energy calculated in this manner was high during overt movements over small regions of pixel space, such as during whisking, while remaining relatively low during passive respiration that corresponded to subtle motion across the animal’s body. A threshold above which an animal was classed as moving was defined on a per-session basis manually. Motion energy distributions, per session, were bimodal, showing a large peak with little variance at low motion energy values and a second, smaller peak with high variance at large values. The threshold was set as the motion energy value separating these two modes. We found that this method of setting the threshold captured both short-duration and long-duration movements. Alternative methods, such as defining a baseline period of no movement against which to compare, were less successful due to the variability in timing of the movements across trials.\\n\\nPhotoinactivation experiments\\n\\nOptogenetic photoinactivation was deployed on approximately 30% of trials selected at random. A ‘masking flash’ (470-mm LEDs; LUXEON Rebel LED) controlled by an Arduino Teensy microcontroller (100-ms pulses at 10 Hz) was delivered constantly for the duration of the session to prevent mice from differentiating control and photoinactivation trials. A 488-nm laser (Coherent, OBIS 488-nm LX 150-mW laser system) was used for all photoinactivation experiments. ChR2-assisted photoinactivation (Fig. 1b,c,g and Extended Data Fig. 1b–d) was performed through a cranial window (see ‘Surgical procedures’ subsection), which replaced bone over the frontal cortex. Light was delivered either at the start of the delay epoch or at the start of the response epoch (only one epoch tested per session). A power density of 1.5 mW mm−2 was used for all photoinactivation experiments.\\n\\nFor delay epoch photoinactivation (Fig. 1g–i and Extended Data Fig. 1b), light was delivered at the onset of the delay epoch and lasted for 0.6 s, followed by a 0.2-s linear ramp down. We targeted one of three regions per session: bilateral motor cortex (ALM and tjM1), bilateral ALM or bilateral tjM1. A scanning galvo system (Thorlabs, GPS011) was used to simultaneously target both hemispheres by scanning at 40 Hz. The beam (2-mm diameter) was centered around the following locations: ALM (AP 2.5 mm, ML 1.5 mm), tjM1 (AP 1.75 mm, ML 2.25 mm) and motor cortex (that is, ALM and tjM1; AP 2.25 mm, ML 1.85 mm). Due to their proximity, when specifically targeting ALM or tjM1, Kwik-Cast (World Precision Instruments) was applied to the surrounding regions to prevent photoinactivation of other regions. For photoinactivation at the go cue or water drop (Fig. 1b,c and Extended Data Fig. 1c,d), light was delivered to the motor cortex for 0.8 s, followed by a 0.2-s ramp down beginning at the go cue/water drop presentation.\\n\\n\\nElectrophysiology recordings\\n\\nExtracellular recordings were performed in the ALM using one of two types of silicon probes: H2 probes, which have two shanks, each with 32 channels with 25-µm spacing (Cambridge Neurotech), or Neuropixels 1.0 (ref. 59), which have one shank and allow recording from 384 channels arranged in a checkerboard pattern (IMEC). For recordings with H2 probes, the 64-channel voltage signals were multiplexed using a Whisper recording system (Neural Circuits, LLC), recorded on a PXI-8133 board (National Instruments) and digitized at 14 bits. The signals were demultiplexed into 64 (for H2 probes) or 384 (for Neuropixels 1.0) voltage traces sampled at 25 kHz and stored for offline analysis.\\n\\nAt least 6 h before recording, a small craniotomy (1–1.5-mm diameter) was made over the ALM (AP 2.5 mm, ML 1.5 mm). Then, 2–5 recordings were performed on a single craniotomy on consecutive days. After inserting the probes between 900 µm and 1,100 µm (New Scale Technologies, MPM System), brain tissue was allowed to settle for at least 5 min before starting recordings. All recordings were made using SpikeGLX (https://github.com/billkarsh/SpikeGLX, phase3a release).\\n\\nBehavioral analysis\\n\\nBehavioral data were collected using Bpod r0.5 (https://www.sanworks.io/shop/products.php?productFamily=bpod). All sessions used for behavioral analysis had at least 40 correct DR trials for each direction (left or right) and 20 correct WC trials for each direction, excluding early lick and ignore trials, which were omitted from all analyses. To assess the impact of ALM photoinactivation on movement initiation, we first calculated the percent of trials with a correct lick within 600 ms of the go cue/water drop (Fig. 1c). To find the fraction of time with the tongue visible during the photoinactivation period (Extended Data Fig. 1d), for each trial we found the number of timepoints within the 1 s after the go cue/water drop where the tongue was labeled as visible by DeepLabCut and divided that by the total number of timepoints within the photoinactivation period. This was then averaged for all control or photoinactivation trials for a given session.\\n\\nTo assess the impact of bilateral motor cortex/ALM/tjM1 photoinactivation during the delay epoch, the average motion energy during the delay epoch was calculated separately for right and left control versus photoinactivation trials (Fig. 1h and Extended Data Fig. 1b).\\n\\nElectrophysiology recording analysis\\n\\nJRCLUST (https://github.com/JaneliaSciComp/JRCLUST, version 4.1.0) and/or Kilosort 3 (ref. 61) (https://github.com/MouseLand/Kilosort) with manual curation using Phy 2 (https://github.com/cortex-lab/phy) were used for spike sorting. A unit was considered a well-isolated single unit based on manual inspection of its inter-spike interval (ISI) histogram, separation from other units and its stationarity across the session. Units that passed manual curation but had a higher ISI violation rate were called multiunits. Recording sessions were included for analysis only if they had at least 10 units (see Extended Data Fig. 2 for a distribution of neuron counts across sessions). For the DR task, we recorded 1,651 units (483 single units) in ALM from 25 sessions using nine mice. In 12 sessions from six mice, animals performed the two-context task. In total, 522 units (214 well-isolated single units) were recorded in these sessions. Finally, for the randomized delay task, we recorded 845 units (288 well-isolated single units) in ALM from 19 sessions using four mice. For subspace alignment (Figs. 5g,n, 7d,h and 8d) and single-unit selectivity analyses (Fig. 3g and Extended Data Fig. 4c), only well-isolated single units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz were included. All units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz were included in all other analyses.\\n\\nTo find choice-selective neurons, 40 trials were subsampled for both right and left correct trials, and a rank-sum test was used to compare spike counts for each unit during the sample, delay or response epochs. Selective units were those with significantly different spike counts (P < 0.05) during a given epoch. Context-selective units were defined in a two-step process to ensure that we were not conflating context selectivity with slow changes in animal engagement/motivation over each session. First, 40 trials each were subsampled for DR and WC trials, and spike counts were compared (P < 0.05, rank-sum test) during the ITI (the 300 ms preceding the sample tone onset) when the animal received no external cues to indicate which context it was in. Because sessions always began with a DR block and often ended with a WC block, it is possible that differences in firing rates across contexts defined in this way could be representing time within the session. To account for this, for each unit identified in the first step, we calculated the difference in spike rate for each pair of DR and WC blocks (for example, a session with five blocks, DR–WC–DR–WC–DR, would have nPairs = 4 adjacent, overlapping block pairs) and included units as context selective only if their preferred context (context with a higher spike rate) was the same for at least nPairs − 1 of the block pairs.\\n\\nSelectivity in the neural population (Extended Data Fig. 4c) was calculated as the difference in spike rate on preferred − non-preferred trials in choice-selective units, with preferred trials referring to the trial type with a higher spike rate for each unit.\\n\",\n   465→              \"numLines\": 59,\n   466→              \"startLine\": 1,\n   467→              \"totalLines\": 59"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01X22FaGh6nodhKsWwtBGCBk","session_id":"57de4683-f8e8-41c0-90c5-1394c0d828d4","uuid":"e3ec8df8-d56e-4b1f-85be-3f65d462f8b9","timestamp":"2026-08-03T14:01:35.795Z"}

When quality='all', returns all clusters except those labeled 'garbage', 'garba', 'noisy', or 'real?'. Otherwise uses pattern matching to find clusters matching specified quality labels.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 6. **alignSpikes.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction obj = alignSpikes(obj,params,prbnum)\\n\\nif strcmp(params.alignEvent,'moveOnset')\\n    obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent) = findMoveOnset(obj);\\nend\\n\\nif strcmp(params.alignEvent,'firstLick')\\n    % get first lick time for left and right licks\\n    temp = obj.bp.ev.lickL;\\n    idx = ~cellfun('isempty',temp);\\n    outL = zeros(size(temp));\\n    outL(idx) = cellfun(@(v)v(1),temp(idx));\\n    temp = obj.bp.ev.lickR;\\n    idx = ~cellfun('isempty',temp);\\n    outR = zeros(size(temp));\\n    outR(idx) = cellfun(@(v)v(1),temp(idx));\\n    firstLick = zeros(size(temp));\\n    % firstLick = min(outL,outR), except when outL||outR == 0\\n    outL(outL==0) = nan;\\n    outR(outR==0) = nan;\\n    firstLick = nanmin(outL,outR);\\n    firstLick(isnan(firstLick)) = 0;\\n    obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent) = firstLick;\\nend\\nif strcmp(params.alignEvent,'lastLick')\\n    % get last lick time for left and right licks\\n    temp = obj.bp.ev.lickL;\\n    idx = ~cellfun('isempty',temp);\\n    outL = zeros(size(temp));\\n    outL(idx) = cellfun(@(v)v(end),temp(idx));\\n    temp = obj.bp.ev.lickR;\\n    idx = ~cellfun('isempty',temp);\\n    outR = zeros(size(temp));\\n    outR(idx) = cellfun(@(v)v(end),temp(idx));\\n    lastLick = zeros(size(temp));\\n    % lastLick = max(outL,outR), except when outL||outR == 0\\n    outL(outL==0) = nan;\\n    outR(outR==0) = nan;\\n    lastLick = nanmax(outL,outR);\\n    lastLick(isnan(lastLick)) = 0;\\n    obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent) = lastLick;\\nend\\n\\nif strcmp(params.alignEvent,'jawOnset')\\n    % half rise time to first peak of jaw onset after go cue\\n    view = 1; % side cam\\n    [~,jawMask] = patternMatchCellArray(obj.traj{view}(1).featNames,{'jaw'},'all');\\n    feat = find(jawMask); % jaw\\n\\n    % filter params\\n    opts.f_cut = 60; % cutoff freq for butter filt\\n    opts.f_n   = 2;  % filter order\\n\\n    % peak finding params\\n    opts.minpkdist = 0.06; % number of ms around peaks to reject peaks\\n    opts.minpkprom = 10;   % a threshold for the peak size\\n    obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent) = alignJawOnset(view,feat,obj,opts);\\nend\\n\\n% align spikes to params.alignEvent\\nfor clu = 1:numel(obj.clu{prbnum})\\n    event = obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent)(obj.clu{prbnum}(clu).trial);\\n\\n    obj.clu{prbnum}(clu).trialtm_aligned = obj.clu{prbnum}(clu).trialtm - event;\\nend\\n\\nend % alignSpikes\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Re-references spike times relative to an alignment event. Handles multiple event types: goCue (already in obj), moveOnset (extracted via findMoveOnset), firstLick/lastLick (computed as minimum/maximum of left/right lick times), and jawOnset (detected via video processing). For each cluster on the specified probe, subtracts the event time from spike times within each trial.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 7. **getSeq.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction obj = getSeq(obj,params,prbnum,varargin)\\n\\nif isfield(obj,'psth')\\n    obj = rmfield(obj,{'psth','trialdat'});\\nend\\n\\nif nargin > 3\\n    doPSTH = varargin{1};\\nelse\\n    doPSTH = true;\\nend\\n\\nedges = params.tmin:params.dt:params.tmax;\\nobj.time = edges + params.dt/2;\\nobj.time = obj.time(1:end-1);\\n\\nspkedges = params.tmin:0.001:params.tmax;\\n\\n% get psths by condition\\nobj.psth{prbnum} = zeros(numel(obj.time),numel(params.cluid{prbnum}),numel(params.condition));\\nif doPSTH\\n    for i = 1:numel(params.cluid{prbnum})\\n        curClu = params.cluid{prbnum}(i);\\n        for j = 1:numel(params.condition)\\n\\n            trix = params.trialid{j};\\n\\n            spkix = ismember(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trial, trix);\\n\\n\\n            % if no spikes found for current set of trials (trix), move on to\\n            % next set\\n            if all(~spkix)\\n                continue\\n            end\\n\\n\\n            N = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm_aligned(spkix), edges);\\n            N = N(1:end-1);\\n\\n            obj.psth{prbnum}(:,i,j) = mySmooth(N./numel(trix)./params.dt, params.smooth, params.bctype);  % trial-averaged separated by trial type\\n\\n        end\\n    end\\nend\\n\\n\\n% get single trial data\\nobj.trialdat{prbnum} = zeros(numel(obj.time),numel(params.cluid{prbnum}),obj.bp.Ntrials);\\n% obj.trialspikes{prbnum} = zeros(numel(spkedges),numel(params.cluid{prbnum}),obj.bp.Ntrials);\\nfor i = 1:numel(params.cluid{prbnum})\\n    curClu = params.cluid{prbnum}(i);\\n    for j = 1:obj.bp.Ntrials\\n\\n        spkix = ismember(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trial, j);\\n\\n        % if no spikes found for current trial (j), move on to\\n        % next trial\\n        if all(~spkix)\\n            continue\\n        end\\n\\n\\n        N = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm_aligned(spkix), edges);\\n        N = N(1:end-1);\\n        if size(N,2) > size(N,1)\\n            N = N'; % make sure N is a column vector\\n        end\\n\\n\\n        obj.trialdat{prbnum}(:,i,j) = mySmooth(N./params.dt,params.smooth, params.bctype);\\n\\n        %         % raw spikes\\n        %         spkN = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm_aligned(spkix), spkedges);\\n        %         if size(spkN,2) > size(spkN,1)\\n        %             spkN = spkN'; % make sure N is a column vector\\n        %         end\\n        %         spkN(spkN > 1) = 1; % binarize (should only have at most 1 spk in 1 ms bin)\\n        %         obj.trialspikes{prbnum}(:,i,j) = spkN;\\n\\n    end\\nend\\n\\nend % getSeq\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Creates binned, smoothed spike data at two levels: (1) PSTH (trial-averaged per condition) with dimensions [time × units × conditions], (2) Single-trial data with dimensions [time × units × trials]. Uses histogram binning with bin width params.dt (default 5ms), normalized by trial count and bin width to get firing rate (spikes/sec). Smooths with Gaussian kernel (params.smooth=15ms) and applies boundary condition handling. Time axis spans params.tmin to params.tmax (default -2.5 to +2.5 sec).\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 8. **getDefaultParams.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction params = getDefaultParams()\\n\\nparams.alignEvent          = 'goCue'; % 'jawOnset' 'goCue'  'moveOnset'  'firstLick'  'lastLick'\\n\\n% time warping only operates on neural data for now.\\n% TODO: time warp for video and bpod data\\nparams.timeWarp            = 0;  % piecewise linear time warping - each lick duration on each trial gets warped to median lick duration for that lick across trials\\nparams.nLicks              = 20; % number of post go cue licks to calculate median lick duration for and warp individual trials to \\n\\nparams.lowFR               = 0.5; % remove clusters with firing rates across all trials less than this val\\n\\n% set conditions to calculate PSTHs for\\nparams.condition(1)     = {'R&hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};         % right hits, no stim, aw off\\nparams.condition(end+1) = {'L&hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};         % left hits, no stim, aw off\\nparams.condition(end+1) = {'R&hit&~stim.enable&autowater&~early'};          % right hits, no stim, aw on\\nparams.condition(end+1) = {'L&hit&~stim.enable&autowater&~early'};          % left hits, no stim, aw on\\n% params.condition(end+1) = {'R&miss&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};        % error right, no stim, aw off\\n% params.condition(end+1) = {'L&miss&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};        % error left, no stim, aw off\\n% params.condition(end+1) = {'~hit&~miss&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};    % ignore\\n% params.condition(end+1) = {'hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};           % hit 2afc\\n% params.condition(end+1) = {'hit&~stim.enable&autowater&~early'};            % hit aw\\n\\n% specify probe number and areas to load and process data\\nparams.probe(1) = 1;\\nparams.probeArea{1} = 'ALM';\\n% \\n% params.probe(end+1) = 2;\\n% params.probeArea{end} = 'ALM';\\n\\nparams.tmin = -2.5;\\nparams.tmax = 2.5;\\nparams.dt = 1/200;\\n\\n% smooth with causal gaussian kernel\\nparams.smooth = 15;\\n\\n% cluster qualities to use\\nparams.quality = {'all'}; % accepts any cell array of strings - special character 'all' returns clusters of any quality\\n\\n% video features to use\\n% cell array for {cam0_features,cam1_features}\\n% params.traj_features = {{'tongue','left_tongue','right_tongue','jaw','trident','nose'},...\\n%                         {'top_tongue','topleft_tongue','bottom_tongue','bottomleft_tongue','top_paw','bottom_paw','jaw'}};\\n\\nparams.traj_features = {{'tongue','left_tongue','right_tongue','jaw','trident','nose'},...\\n                        {'top_tongue','topleft_tongue','bottom_tongue','bottomleft_tongue','jaw','top_paw','bottom_paw','top_nostril','bottom_nostril'}};\\n\\nparams.feat_varToExplain = 80; % num factors for dim reduction of video features should explain this much variance\\n\\n\\n% --SPECIFY TIME POINTS AND LAG TO USE\\nparams.prep = [-2.5 -0.05]; % initial and final time points (seconds) defining prep epoch, relative to alignevent\\nparams.move = [-2.5 1.5];   % initial and final time points (seconds) defining move epoch, relative to alignevent\\n% params.advance_movement = 0.025; % seconds, amount of time to advance movement data relative to neural data\\nparams.advance_movement = 0.0;\\n\\nend\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Sets default parameters for data processing. Key parameters: alignment event (goCue), firing rate threshold (0.5 Hz), time window (-2.5 to +2.5 sec), bin size (5 ms, dt=1/200), smoothing (15 ms Gaussian), trial conditions (right/left hits, with/without autowater, excluding early licks). Includes video feature lists and epoch definitions (prep: -2.5 to -0.05 sec; move: -2.5 to +1.5 sec).\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 9. **baselineFR.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction [presampleFR, presampleSigma] = baselineFR(obj,params,prbnum)\\n\\n% baselineFR - (nCells,nTrialTypes) mean FR for each clu and tt\\n\\nedges = -0.5:params.dt:mode(obj.bp.ev.sample);\\ntime = edges + params.dt/2;\\ntime = time(1:end-1);\\n\\n% get psths by condition\\npsth = zeros(numel(time),numel(params.cluid{prbnum}),numel(params.condition));\\nfor i = 1:numel(params.cluid{prbnum})\\n    curClu = params.cluid{prbnum}(i);\\n    for j = 1:numel(params.condition)\\n        trix = params.trialid{j};\\n        spkix = ismember(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trial, trix);\\n\\n        N = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm(spkix), edges);\\n        N = N(1:end-1);\\n\\n        psth(:,i,j) = mySmooth(N./numel(trix)./params.dt, params.smooth);  % trial-averaged separated by trial type\\n    end\\nend\\n\\npresampleFR = squeeze(median(psth,1));\\npresampleSigma = squeeze(std(psth,[],1));\\n\\nend\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Calculates baseline firing rate statistics during the presample epoch (from -0.5 sec to sample event time). For each cluster and condition, bins spikes with smoothing. Returns median firing rate and standard deviation across time points, resulting in arrays of shape [nUnits × nConditions]. Used for baseline normalization.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 10. **removeLowFRClusters.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction [obj,cluid] = removeLowFRClusters(obj,cluid,lowFR,prbnum)\\n% % Remove low-firing rate units, e.g., all those firing less than lowFR\\n%   spikes per second on average across all trials.\\n%   \\n%   The fitted observation noise (diagonal element of R) for a\\n%   low-firing rate unit will be small, so the neural trajectory may\\n%   show a deflection each time the neuron spikes.\\n\\nmeanFRs = mean(mean(obj.psth{prbnum},3,'omitnan'),'omitnan');\\nuse = meanFRs > lowFR;\\n\\n% remove low fr clusters\\ncluid = cluid(use);\\nobj.psth{prbnum} = obj.psth{prbnum}(:,use,:);\\nobj.trialdat{prbnum} = obj.trialdat{prbnum}(:,use,:);\\n\\nend % removeLowFRClusters\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Filters out units with mean firing rate below the lowFR threshold (default 0.5 Hz, can be set to 1 Hz). Computes mean firing rate by averaging the PSTH across time and conditions. Removes matching clusters from cluid array and prunes corresponding columns from psth and trialdat arrays.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 11. **loadKinData.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction temp = loadKinData(meta,runix)\\n\\npth = meta.datapth;\\nanm = meta.anm;\\ndate = meta.date;\\n\\npth = fullfile(pth,'kinematics');\\n\\nanm_date = [anm '_' date];\\n\\nif isempty(runix)\\n    runix = getMostRecentRun(fullfile(pth,'input'),anm_date);\\nend\\n\\ninfn = ['kin_' anm_date '_' runix '.mat'];\\n\\ntemp = load(fullfile(pth,infn));\\n\\nend\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Loads kinematics data from file `kin_[animal]_[date]_[runix].mat` stored in datapth/kinematics/. Dataset from mice (mus musculus) performing delayed-response and water-cued licking tasks with recordings from anterior lateral motor cortex (ALM). Data downloadable from Zenodo. Data structure: `data_structure_Animal_SessionDate.mat` with neural spikes, task info, and DeepLabCut video output; motion energy stored separately in `motionEnergy_Animal_SessionDate.mat`. Sessions contain either full ephys+behavior or behavior only (inhibition sessions). Reference WorkingWithDataObjs.m for usage examples.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## CONSOLIDATED SUMMARY\\n\\n**Data Loading & Structure:**\\n- Raw data objects contain: behavior (obj.bp), spike data (obj.clu, organized by probe), kinematics (obj.traj from DeepLabCut), event times (obj.bp.ev)\\n- loadObjs loads mat files; assignDataPath handles path assignment\\n- loadKinData and loadMotionEnergy load kinematic/video-based features separately\\n\\n**Neural Data Filtering:**\\n1. **Unit Selection (findClusters):** Filters by quality label (default='all' excludes garbage/noisy)\\n2. **Firing Rate Filtering (removeLowFRClusters):** Removes units with mean FR < 0.5 Hz\\n\\n**Trial Filtering (findTrials):**\\n- Evaluates logical conditions on obj.bp fields (R, L, hit, miss, early, stim.enable, autowater, etc.)\\n- Returns trial indices for each condition\\n- Handles early-lick trials separately (fidx field)\\n\\n**Time Alignment (alignSpikes):**\\n- Aligns spike times to reference events: goCue, moveOnset, firstLick, lastLick, jawOnset\\n- Supports custom event detection (jaw movement from video)\\n- Creates trialtm_aligned = trialtm - alignmentEventTime for each cluster per trial\\n\\n**Data Processing Pipeline:**\\n1. findTrials → identify valid trials per condition\\n2. findClusters → identify valid units by quality\\n3. alignSpikes → re-reference to alignment event\\n4. getSeq → bin spikes (5ms default), smooth (15ms Gaussian), compute PSTHs and single-trial data\\n5. removeLowFRClusters → filter units by mean firing rate\\n6. baselineFR → calculate presample baseline (median FR and std during -0.5 to sample event)\\n\\n**Output Data Structures:**\\n- obj.psth: [time × units × conditions] trial-averaged firing rate\\n- obj.trialdat: [time × units × trials] single-trial binned firing rate\\n- obj.time: time axis (-2.5 to +2.5 sec default, 5ms bins)\\n- obj.presampleFR, obj.presampleSigma: baseline statistics [units × conditions]\\n\\n**Kinematic/Motion Data:**\\n- Motion energy loaded at 400 Hz, interpolated to neural data time resolution\\n- Binary movement detection via threshold (me.moveThresh)\\n- Video offset correction via findVideoOffset\\n- Kinematic features reduced via dimensionality reduction\"\n   943→              }\n   944→            ],"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01X22FaGh6nodhKsWwtBGCBk","session_id":"57de4683-f8e8-41c0-90c5-1394c0d828d4","uuid":"e4947325-7923-4525-9066-848c379f56cd","timestamp":"2026-08-03T14:02:00.157Z"}

If 1,\\n% don't process clusters\\nif nargin > 3\\n    behav_only = varargin{1};\\nelse\\n    behav_only = 0;\\nend\\n\\n%% STANDARD ROUTINES\\n% find trials to use (only need to do this once)\\nif prbnum==1 || ~isfield(params,'trialid')\\n    params.trialid = findTrials(obj, params.condition);\\n    disp(' ')\\n    disp('--Trials Found')\\n    disp(' ')\\nend\\n\\n% find clusters to use\\nparams.cluid{prbnum} = findClusters({obj.clu{prbnum}(:).quality}', params.quality);\\ndisp(' ')\\ndisp('--Clusters Found')\\ndisp(' ')\\n\\nif behav_only\\n    return\\nend\\n\\n% align spikes in every cluster to an event\\nobj = alignSpikes(obj,params,prbnum);\\ndisp(' ')\\ndisp('--Spikes Aligned')\\ndisp(' ')\\n\\n% get trial avg psth and single trial data\\nif ~isfield(params,'bctype') % boundary condition for smoothing\\n    params.bctype = 'none';\\nend\\nobj = getSeq(obj,params,prbnum);\\ndisp(' ')\\ndisp('--PSTHs and Single Trial Data Done')\\ndisp(' ')\\n\\n% remove low fr clusters\\n[obj, params.cluid{prbnum}] = removeLowFRClusters(obj,params.cluid{prbnum},params.lowFR,prbnum);\\nparams.probeid{prbnum} = ones(size(params.cluid{prbnum})) * prbnum;\\ndisp(' ')\\ndisp('--Removed Low Firing Rate Clusters')\\ndisp(' ')\\n\\n% get mean fr and std dev for each cluster/trial type during presample (baseline fr)\\n[obj.presampleFR{prbnum}, obj.presampleSigma{prbnum}] = baselineFR(obj,params,prbnum);\\ndisp(' ')\\ndisp('--Presample Statisitics Calculated')\\ndisp(' ')\\n\\nend\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Main processing pipeline for a single probe. Orchestrates the sequence: (1) Find valid trials based on conditions, (2) Find clusters by quality, (3) Align spikes to a reference event, (4) Generate PSTHs and single-trial data, (5) Remove low-firing-rate units, (6) Calculate baseline firing rate statistics during presample period.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 4. **findTrials.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction trialNums = findTrials(obj, conditions)\\n\\n% % older data objects have obj.bp.autowater.nums\\n% % newer data objects do not (from summer2021 pipeline)\\n% if ~isfield(obj.bp.autowater, 'nums')\\n%     tmp = obj.bp.autowater;\\n%     obj.bp = rmfield(obj.bp, 'autowater');\\n%     obj.bp.autowater.nums = tmp + (tmp-1)*-2;\\n% end\\n\\nvarnames = getStructVarNames(obj);\\nNtrials = obj.bp.Ntrials;\\nfor i = 1:numel(varnames)\\n    eval([varnames{i} ' = obj.bp.' varnames{i} ';']);\\n    \\n    if eval(['numel(' varnames{i} ')==obj.bp.Ntrials && isrow(' varnames{i} ')'])\\n        eval([varnames{i} '=' varnames{i} ''';']);\\n    end\\n    \\n    eval([varnames{i} '=' varnames{i} '(~isnan(' varnames{i} '))' ''';'])\\nend\\n\\nif isfield (obj.bp, 'fidx')\\n    obj.bp.Ntrials = sum(obj.bp.fidx==2);\\n    addToTrialNums = sum(obj.bp.fidx==1);\\nend\\n\\nmask = zeros(obj.bp.Ntrials, numel(conditions));\\n\\nfor i = 1:numel(conditions)\\n    try\\n        mask(:,i) = eval(conditions{i});\\n    catch\\n        mask(:,i) = eval(conditions{i}{1});\\n    end\\n    if exist('addToTrialNums','var') == 1\\n        trialNums{i} = find(mask(:,i)) + addToTrialNums;\\n    else\\n        trialNums{i} = find(mask(:,i));\\n    end\\nend\\n\\nend % findTrials\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Filters trials based on condition strings (stored in obj.bp). For each condition, evaluates logical expressions on behavioral variables (hit, miss, R, L, stim.enable, autowater, early, etc.) and returns trial indices matching each condition. Handles a fidx field for distinguishing early vs. valid lick trials.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 5. **findClusters.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction idx = findClusters(qualityList, qualities)\\n% find idx where qualityList contains at least one of the patterns in\\n% qualities\\n\\n% remove leading/trailing spaces\\n% also check if each entry in qualityList is a string/char, if not, change\\n% it\\nfor i = 1:numel(qualityList)\\n    if ~ischar(qualityList{i}) && ~isstring(qualityList{i})\\n        qualityList{i} = '';\\n    end\\nend\\nqualityList = strtrim(qualityList);\\n\\nif any(strcmpi(qualities,'all')) || strcmpi(qualities{1},'all')\\n    idx = find(~ismember(qualityList,'garbage') & ~ismember(qualityList,'gabrga') & ~ismember(qualityList,'noisy') & ~ismember(qualityList,'real?'));\\n%     idx = 1:numel(qualityList);\\n    return\\nend\\n\\n% handle unlabeled cluster qualities\\nfor i = 1:numel(qualityList)\\n    if isempty(qualityList{i})\\n        qualityList(i) = {'nan'};\\n    end\\n%     qualityList(i) = lower(qualityList(i));\\nend\\n\\n[~,mask] = patternMatchCellArray(qualityList, qualities, 'any');\\n\\nidx = find(mask);\\nend % findClusters\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Filters units by quality. When quality='all', returns all clusters except those labeled 'garbage', 'garba', 'noisy', or 'real?'. Otherwise uses pattern matching to find clusters matching specified quality labels.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 6. **alignSpikes.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction obj = alignSpikes(obj,params,prbnum)\\n\\nif strcmp(params.alignEvent,'moveOnset')\\n    obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent) = findMoveOnset(obj);\\nend\\n\\nif strcmp(params.alignEvent,'firstLick')\\n    % get first lick time for left and right licks\\n    temp = obj.bp.ev.lickL;\\n    idx = ~cellfun('isempty',temp);\\n    outL = zeros(size(temp));\\n    outL(idx) = cellfun(@(v)v(1),temp(idx));\\n    temp = obj.bp.ev.lickR;\\n    idx = ~cellfun('isempty',temp);\\n    outR = zeros(size(temp));\\n    outR(idx) = cellfun(@(v)v(1),temp(idx));\\n    firstLick = zeros(size(temp));\\n    % firstLick = min(outL,outR), except when outL||outR == 0\\n    outL(outL==0) = nan;\\n    outR(outR==0) = nan;\\n    firstLick = nanmin(outL,outR);\\n    firstLick(isnan(firstLick)) = 0;\\n    obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent) = firstLick;\\nend\\nif strcmp(params.alignEvent,'lastLick')\\n    % get last lick time for left and right licks\\n    temp = obj.bp.ev.lickL;\\n    idx = ~cellfun('isempty',temp);\\n    outL = zeros(size(temp));\\n    outL(idx) = cellfun(@(v)v(end),temp(idx));\\n    temp = obj.bp.ev.lickR;\\n    idx = ~cellfun('isempty',temp);\\n    outR = zeros(size(temp));\\n    outR(idx) = cellfun(@(v)v(end),temp(idx));\\n    lastLick = zeros(size(temp));\\n    % lastLick = max(outL,outR), except when outL||outR == 0\\n    outL(outL==0) = nan;\\n    outR(outR==0) = nan;\\n    lastLick = nanmax(outL,outR);\\n    lastLick(isnan(lastLick)) = 0;\\n    obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent) = lastLick;\\nend\\n\\nif strcmp(params.alignEvent,'jawOnset')\\n    % half rise time to first peak of jaw onset after go cue\\n    view = 1; % side cam\\n    [~,jawMask] = patternMatchCellArray(obj.traj{view}(1).featNames,{'jaw'},'all');\\n    feat = find(jawMask); % jaw\\n\\n    % filter params\\n    opts.f_cut = 60; % cutoff freq for butter filt\\n    opts.f_n   = 2;  % filter order\\n\\n    % peak finding params\\n    opts.minpkdist = 0.06; % number of ms around peaks to reject peaks\\n    opts.minpkprom = 10;   % a threshold for the peak size\\n    obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent) = alignJawOnset(view,feat,obj,opts);\\nend\\n\\n% align spikes to params.alignEvent\\nfor clu = 1:numel(obj.clu{prbnum})\\n    event = obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent)(obj.clu{prbnum}(clu).trial);\\n\\n    obj.clu{prbnum}(clu).trialtm_aligned = obj.clu{prbnum}(clu).trialtm - event;\\nend\\n\\nend % alignSpikes\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Re-references spike times relative to an alignment event. Handles multiple event types: goCue (already in obj), moveOnset (extracted via findMoveOnset), firstLick/lastLick (computed as minimum/maximum of left/right lick times), and jawOnset (detected via video processing). For each cluster on the specified probe, subtracts the event time from spike times within each trial.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 7. **getSeq.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction obj = getSeq(obj,params,prbnum,varargin)\\n\\nif isfield(obj,'psth')\\n    obj = rmfield(obj,{'psth','trialdat'});\\nend\\n\\nif nargin > 3\\n    doPSTH = varargin{1};\\nelse\\n    doPSTH = true;\\nend\\n\\nedges = params.tmin:params.dt:params.tmax;\\nobj.time = edges + params.dt/2;\\nobj.time = obj.time(1:end-1);\\n\\nspkedges = params.tmin:0.001:params.tmax;\\n\\n% get psths by condition\\nobj.psth{prbnum} = zeros(numel(obj.time),numel(params.cluid{prbnum}),numel(params.condition));\\nif doPSTH\\n    for i = 1:numel(params.cluid{prbnum})\\n        curClu = params.cluid{prbnum}(i);\\n        for j = 1:numel(params.condition)\\n\\n            trix = params.trialid{j};\\n\\n            spkix = ismember(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trial, trix);\\n\\n\\n            % if no spikes found for current set of trials (trix), move on to\\n            % next set\\n            if all(~spkix)\\n                continue\\n            end\\n\\n\\n            N = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm_aligned(spkix), edges);\\n            N = N(1:end-1);\\n\\n            obj.psth{prbnum}(:,i,j) = mySmooth(N./numel(trix)./params.dt, params.smooth, params.bctype);  % trial-averaged separated by trial type\\n\\n        end\\n    end\\nend\\n\\n\\n% get single trial data\\nobj.trialdat{prbnum} = zeros(numel(obj.time),numel(params.cluid{prbnum}),obj.bp.Ntrials);\\n% obj.trialspikes{prbnum} = zeros(numel(spkedges),numel(params.cluid{prbnum}),obj.bp.Ntrials);\\nfor i = 1:numel(params.cluid{prbnum})\\n    curClu = params.cluid{prbnum}(i);\\n    for j = 1:obj.bp.Ntrials\\n\\n        spkix = ismember(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trial, j);\\n\\n        % if no spikes found for current trial (j), move on to\\n        % next trial\\n        if all(~spkix)\\n            continue\\n        end\\n\\n\\n        N = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm_aligned(spkix), edges);\\n        N = N(1:end-1);\\n        if size(N,2) > size(N,1)\\n            N = N'; % make sure N is a column vector\\n        end\\n\\n\\n        obj.trialdat{prbnum}(:,i,j) = mySmooth(N./params.dt,params.smooth, params.bctype);\\n\\n        %         % raw spikes\\n        %         spkN = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm_aligned(spkix), spkedges);\\n        %         if size(spkN,2) > size(spkN,1)\\n        %             spkN = spkN'; % make sure N is a column vector\\n        %         end\\n        %         spkN(spkN > 1) = 1; % binarize (should only have at most 1 spk in 1 ms bin)\\n        %         obj.trialspikes{prbnum}(:,i,j) = spkN;\\n\\n    end\\nend\\n\\nend % getSeq\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Creates binned, smoothed spike data at two levels: (1) PSTH (trial-averaged per condition) with dimensions [time × units × conditions], (2) Single-trial data with dimensions [time × units × trials]. Uses histogram binning with bin width params.dt (default 5ms), normalized by trial count and bin width to get firing rate (spikes/sec). Smooths with Gaussian kernel (params.smooth=15ms) and applies boundary condition handling. Time axis spans params.tmin to params.tmax (default -2.5 to +2.5 sec).\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 8. **getDefaultParams.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction params = getDefaultParams()\\n\\nparams.alignEvent          = 'goCue'; % 'jawOnset' 'goCue'  'moveOnset'  'firstLick'  'lastLick'\\n\\n% time warping only operates on neural data for now.\\n% TODO: time warp for video and bpod data\\nparams.timeWarp            = 0;  % piecewise linear time warping - each lick duration on each trial gets warped to median lick duration for that lick across trials\\nparams.nLicks              = 20; % number of post go cue licks to calculate median lick duration for and warp individual trials to \\n\\nparams.lowFR               = 0.5; % remove clusters with firing rates across all trials less than this val\\n\\n% set conditions to calculate PSTHs for\\nparams.condition(1)     = {'R&hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};         % right hits, no stim, aw off\\nparams.condition(end+1) = {'L&hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};         % left hits, no stim, aw off\\nparams.condition(end+1) = {'R&hit&~stim.enable&autowater&~early'};          % right hits, no stim, aw on\\nparams.condition(end+1) = {'L&hit&~stim.enable&autowater&~early'};          % left hits, no stim, aw on\\n% params.condition(end+1) = {'R&miss&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};        % error right, no stim, aw off\\n% params.condition(end+1) = {'L&miss&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};        % error left, no stim, aw off\\n% params.condition(end+1) = {'~hit&~miss&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};    % ignore\\n% params.condition(end+1) = {'hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};           % hit 2afc\\n% params.condition(end+1) = {'hit&~stim.enable&autowater&~early'};            % hit aw\\n\\n% specify probe number and areas to load and process data\\nparams.probe(1) = 1;\\nparams.probeArea{1} = 'ALM';\\n% \\n% params.probe(end+1) = 2;\\n% params.probeArea{end} = 'ALM';\\n\\nparams.tmin = -2.5;\\nparams.tmax = 2.5;\\nparams.dt = 1/200;\\n\\n% smooth with causal gaussian kernel\\nparams.smooth = 15;\\n\\n% cluster qualities to use\\nparams.quality = {'all'}; % accepts any cell array of strings - special character 'all' returns clusters of any quality\\n\\n% video features to use\\n% cell array for {cam0_features,cam1_features}\\n% params.traj_features = {{'tongue','left_tongue','right_tongue','jaw','trident','nose'},...\\n%                         {'top_tongue','topleft_tongue','bottom_tongue','bottomleft_tongue','top_paw','bottom_paw','jaw'}};\\n\\nparams.traj_features = {{'tongue','left_tongue','right_tongue','jaw','trident','nose'},...\\n                        {'top_tongue','topleft_tongue','bottom_tongue','bottomleft_tongue','jaw','top_paw','bottom_paw','top_nostril','bottom_nostril'}};\\n\\nparams.feat_varToExplain = 80; % num factors for dim reduction of video features should explain this much variance\\n\\n\\n% --SPECIFY TIME POINTS AND LAG TO USE\\nparams.prep = [-2.5 -0.05]; % initial and final time points (seconds) defining prep epoch, relative to alignevent\\nparams.move = [-2.5 1.5];   % initial and final time points (seconds) defining move epoch, relative to alignevent\\n% params.advance_movement = 0.025; % seconds, amount of time to advance movement data relative to neural data\\nparams.advance_movement = 0.0;\\n\\nend\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Sets default parameters for data processing. Key parameters: alignment event (goCue), firing rate threshold (0.5 Hz), time window (-2.5 to +2.5 sec), bin size (5 ms, dt=1/200), smoothing (15 ms Gaussian), trial conditions (right/left hits, with/without autowater, excluding early licks). Includes video feature lists and epoch definitions (prep: -2.5 to -0.05 sec; move: -2.5 to +1.5 sec).\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 9. **baselineFR.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction [presampleFR, presampleSigma] = baselineFR(obj,params,prbnum)\\n\\n% baselineFR - (nCells,nTrialTypes) mean FR for each clu and tt\\n\\nedges = -0.5:params.dt:mode(obj.bp.ev.sample);\\ntime = edges + params.dt/2;\\ntime = time(1:end-1);\\n\\n% get psths by condition\\npsth = zeros(numel(time),numel(params.cluid{prbnum}),numel(params.condition));\\nfor i = 1:numel(params.cluid{prbnum})\\n    curClu = params.cluid{prbnum}(i);\\n    for j = 1:numel(params.condition)\\n        trix = params.trialid{j};\\n        spkix = ismember(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trial, trix);\\n\\n        N = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm(spkix), edges);\\n        N = N(1:end-1);\\n\\n        psth(:,i,j) = mySmooth(N./numel(trix)./params.dt, params.smooth);  % trial-averaged separated by trial type\\n    end\\nend\\n\\npresampleFR = squeeze(median(psth,1));\\npresampleSigma = squeeze(std(psth,[],1));\\n\\nend\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Calculates baseline firing rate statistics during the presample epoch (from -0.5 sec to sample event time). For each cluster and condition, bins spikes with smoothing. Returns median firing rate and standard deviation across time points, resulting in arrays of shape [nUnits × nConditions]. Used for baseline normalization.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 10. **removeLowFRClusters.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction [obj,cluid] = removeLowFRClusters(obj,cluid,lowFR,prbnum)\\n% % Remove low-firing rate units, e.g., all those firing less than lowFR\\n%   spikes per second on average across all trials.\\n%   \\n%   The fitted observation noise (diagonal element of R) for a\\n%   low-firing rate unit will be small, so the neural trajectory may\\n%   show a deflection each time the neuron spikes.\\n\\nmeanFRs = mean(mean(obj.psth{prbnum},3,'omitnan'),'omitnan');\\nuse = meanFRs > lowFR;\\n\\n% remove low fr clusters\\ncluid = cluid(use);\\nobj.psth{prbnum} = obj.psth{prbnum}(:,use,:);\\nobj.trialdat{prbnum} = obj.trialdat{prbnum}(:,use,:);\\n\\nend % removeLowFRClusters\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Filters out units with mean firing rate below the lowFR threshold (default 0.5 Hz, can be set to 1 Hz). Computes mean firing rate by averaging the PSTH across time and conditions. Removes matching clusters from cluid array and prunes corresponding columns from psth and trialdat arrays.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 11. **loadKinData.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction temp = loadKinData(meta,runix)\\n\\npth = meta.datapth;\\nanm = meta.anm;\\ndate = meta.date;\\n\\npth = fullfile(pth,'kinematics');\\n\\nanm_date = [anm '_' date];\\n\\nif isempty(runix)\\n    runix = getMostRecentRun(fullfile(pth,'input'),anm_date);\\nend\\n\\ninfn = ['kin_' anm_date '_' runix '.mat'];\\n\\ntemp = load(fullfile(pth,infn));\\n\\nend\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Loads kinematics data from file `kin_[animal]_[date]_[runix].mat` stored in datapth/kinematics/. Takes animal name, date, and run index as inputs. If no run index provided, defaults to the most recent run. Returns the loaded structure containing kinematic features.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 12. **loadMotionEnergy.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction me = loadMotionEnergy(obj,meta,params,datapth,varargin)\\n\\nif nargin > 4\\n    returnAfterLoad = varargin{1};\\nelse\\n    returnAfterLoad = 0;\\nend\\n\\n% %------------------------------------------------------------\\n% % --first check to see if obj has an 'me' and has thresh--\\n% % -----------------------------------------------------------\\n% if isfield(obj,'me')\\n%     if isstruct(obj.me) && ~iscell(obj.me.data)\\n%         obj.me.move = obj.me.data > (obj.me.moveThresh);\\n%         me = obj.me;\\n%         return\\n%     end\\n% else\\n% -----------------------------------------------------------\\n% -- if not, loadMotionEnergy, and save it it to obj.me--\\n% -----------------------------------------------------------\\n\\n% find and load motion energy .mat file\\nmepth = fullfile(datapth,'DataObjects',meta.anm);\\ncontents = dir(mepth);\\ncontents = contents(~[contents.isdir]);\\n\\nfns = {contents.name}';\\n\\nfn = patternMatchCellArray({contents.name}',{'motionEnergy',meta.date},'all');\\n\\nif numel(fn) == 1\\n    fn = fn{1};\\nelseif numel(fn) == 2\\n    ix = ~contains(fn,'._'); % on windows, sometimes a file that starts with '._motionEnergy*' is found\\n    fn = fn{ix};\\nelse\\n    disp('UNABLE TO LOCATE A MOTION ENERGY FILE IN: ')\\n    disp(mepth)\\n    disp('      Continuing without motion energy as a feature')\\n    me.use = 0;\\n    me.data = nan;\\n    me.moveThresh = nan;\\n    return\\nend\\n\\ntemp = load(fullfile(mepth,fn));\\nme = temp.me;\\n\\nif isstruct(me.data)\\n    me.data = me.data.data;\\nend\\n\\nif returnAfterLoad\\n    return\\nend\\n\\nvidshift = findVideoOffset(obj);\\n% vidshift = 1;\\n\\n% -----------------------------------------------------------\\n% trim trial length (me.data contains motion energy for each time point in\\n% trial at 400 Hz). Want to align to params.alignEvent and want to put it\\n% in same dt as neural data\\n% -----------------------------------------------------------\\ntaxis = obj.time + params.advance_movement;\\nalignTimes = obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent);\\nme.newdata = zeros(numel(obj.time),numel(me.data));\\nfor trix = 1:numel(me.data)\\n    try\\n        me.newdata(:,trix) = interp1(obj.traj{1}(trix).frameTimes-vidshift-alignTimes(trix),me.data{trix},taxis); % interp1(old_time,me,new_time);\\n    catch % if frameTimes doesn't exist or is full of NaNs - shouldn't be dummy data as we aren't using those sessions\\n        frameTimes = (1:size(obj.traj{1}(trix).ts,1)) ./ 400;\\n        me.newdata(:,trix) = interp1(frameTimes-0.5-alignTimes(trix),me.data{trix},taxis);\\n    end\\nend\\n\\n% replace me.data with me.newdata\\nme.data = me.newdata;\\nme = rmfield(me,'newdata');\\n% fill nans with nearest value (there are some nans at the start of each\\n% trial)\\nme.data = fillmissing(me.data,'nearest');\\n\\n% -------------------------------------------------------------------\\n% -- assign move time points as logical array same size as me.data --\\n% -------------------------------------------------------------------\\nme.move = me.data > (me.moveThresh);\\n\\n% % baseline subtract \\n% ps = [mode(obj.bp.ev.bitStart) mode(obj.bp.ev.sample-obj.bp.ev.bitStart)] - mode(obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent));\\n% for i = 1:numel(ps)\\n%     [~,psix(i)] = min(abs(obj.time - ps(i)));\\n% end\\n% psme = mean(me.data(psix(1):psix(2),:),1);\\n% me.data = me.data - psme;\\n\\nend % loadMotionEnergy\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Loads motion energy data from file `motionEnergy_[animal]_[date].mat` in datapth/DataObjects/[animal]/. Original data is at 400 Hz, single per trial. Resamples/interpolates to match neural data time axis using alignment event timing. Finds video offset and aligns to the specified alignEvent (e.g., goCue). Creates binary movement detection by thresholding (me.move = me.data > me.moveThresh). Handles missing values by forward-filling.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 13. **loadProcessedData.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction [obj,fa,gpfa,params,me,kin,kinfeats,kinfeats_reduced] = loadProcessedData(meta)\\n\\nrun = 'run4';\\n\\nuse = true(size(meta));\\nfor i = 1:numel(meta)\\n    disp(['Loading data for ' meta(i).anm ' ' meta(i).date]);\\n\\n%     [meta(i),params(i),obj(i),dat(i)] = getNeuralActivity(meta(i),dfparams);\\n    tempfa = getFAData(meta(i),run);\\n\\n    params(i) = tempfa.params;\\n\\n    if isfield(tempfa.obj,'meta')\\n        tempfa.obj = rmfield(tempfa.obj,'meta');\\n    end\\n    if isfield(tempfa.obj,'ex')\\n        tempfa.obj = rmfield(tempfa.obj,'ex');\\n    end\\n        \\n    obj(i) = tempfa.obj;\\n\\n    me(i) = loadMotionEnergy(obj(i),meta(i),params(i),1:obj(i).bp.Ntrials); \\n    if ~me(i).use\\n        use(i) = false;\\n    end\\n\\n    fa(i) = rmfield(tempfa,{'meta','obj','params'});\\n\\n    kinematics = loadKinData(meta(i),run);\\n    kin(i) = kinematics.kin;\\n    kinfeats{i} = kinematics.kinfeats;\\n    kinfeats_reduced{i} = kinematics.kinfeats_reduced;\\n\\n    me(i).moveIx = getMoveIndex(me(i),kin(i),kinfeats{i});\\n\\n\\n    tempgpfa = getGPFAData(meta(i),run);\\n    gpfa(i) = rmfield(tempgpfa,{'meta','obj','params'});\\n\\n    disp('DONE');\\n    disp(' ');\\nend\\n\\nfa = fa(use);\\nparams = params(use);\\nmeta = meta(use);\\nobj = obj(use);\\nme = me(use);\\nkin = kin(use);\\nkinfeats = kinfeats(use);\\nkinfeats_reduced = kinfeats_reduced(use);\\n\\nend\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** High-level loader for processed data. Loads from run='run4' subdirectories. For each session: (1) loads FA (factor analysis) data via getFAData, (2) loads motion energy with loadMotionEnergy, (3) loads kinematics and kinematic features with loadKinData, (4) computes moveIx index linking motion energy to kinematics, (5) loads GPFA data. Filters out sessions where motion energy is unavailable (me.use==false). Returns: obj (neural data), fa (factor analysis results), gpfa (Gaussian process factor analysis), params, me (motion energy), kin (kinematics), kinfeats, kinfeats_reduced.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 14. **loadGPFAData.m**\\n**Full Content:**\\n```matlab\\nfunction gpfa = loadGPFAData(meta)\\n\\nrun = 'run4';\\n\\nuse = true(size(meta));\\nfor i = 1:numel(meta)\\n    disp(['Loading data for ' meta(i).anm ' ' meta(i).date]);\\n\\n    tempfa = getFAData(meta(i),run);\\n\\n    params(i) = tempfa.params;\\n\\n    if isfield(tempfa.obj,'meta')\\n        tempfa.obj = rmfield(tempfa.obj,'meta');\\n    end\\n    if isfield(tempfa.obj,'ex')\\n        tempfa.obj = rmfield(tempfa.obj,'ex');\\n    end\\n        \\n    obj(i) = tempfa.obj;\\n\\n    me(i) = loadMotionEnergy(obj(i),meta(i),params(i),1:obj(i).bp.Ntrials); \\n    if ~me(i).use\\n        use(i) = false;\\n    end\\n\\n    fa(i) = rmfield(tempfa,{'meta','obj','params'});\\n\\n    kinematics = loadKinData(meta(i),run);\\n    kin(i) = kinematics.kin;\\n    kinfeats{i} = kinematics.kinfeats;\\n    kinfeats_reduced{i} = kinematics.kinfeats_reduced;\\n\\n    me(i).moveIx = getMoveIndex(me(i),kin(i),kinfeats{i});\\n\\n    disp('DONE');\\n    disp(' ');\\nend\\n\\nfa = fa(use);\\nparams = params(use);\\nmeta = meta(use);\\nobj = obj(use);\\nme = me(use);\\nkin = kin(use);\\nkinfeats = kinfeats(use);\\nkinfeats_reduced = kinfeats_reduced(use);\\n\\nend\\n```\\n\\n**Summary:** Appears to be nearly identical to loadProcessedData.m but only returns gpfa (Gaussian process factor analysis). % 'jawOnset' 'goCue'  'moveOnset'  'firstLick'  'lastLick'\n\nparams.timeWarp            = 0;\nparams.nLicks              = 20;\n\nparams.lowFR               = 0.5; % remove clusters with firing rates less than this val\n\n% set conditions to calculate PSTHs for\nparams.condition(1)     = {'R&hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};   % right hits, no stim, aw off\nparams.condition(end+1) = {'L&hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};   % left hits, no stim, aw off\nparams.condition(end+1) = {'R&hit&~stim.enable&autowater&~early'};    % right hits, no stim, aw on\nparams.condition(end+1) = {'L&hit&~stim.enable&autowater&~early'};    % left hits, no stim, aw on\n\nparams.probe(1) = 1;\nparams.probeArea{1} = 'ALM';\n\nparams.tmin = -2.5;\nparams.tmax = 2.5;\nparams.dt = 1/200;   % 5 ms bins\n\nparams.smooth = 15;  % 15 ms Gaussian kernel\n\nparams.quality = {'all'};\n\nparams.traj_features = {{'tongue','left_tongue','right_tongue','jaw','trident','nose'},...\n                        {'top_tongue','topleft_tongue','bottom_tongue','bottomleft_tongue','jaw','top_paw','bottom_paw','top_nostril','bottom_nostril'}};\n\nparams.feat_varToExplain = 80;\n\nparams.prep = [-2.5 -0.05];  % prep epoch (s) relative to goCue\nparams.move = [-2.5 1.5];    % move epoch (s) relative to goCue\nparams.advance_movement = 0.0;\nend\n```\n\n**Key parameters:**\n- Alignment: `goCue`\n- Time window: -2.5 to +2.5 s, at 5 ms bins (dt = 1/200)\n- Firing rate threshold: 0.5 Hz\n- Smoothing: 15 ms causal Gaussian kernel\n- Quality filter: 'all' (excludes garbage/noisy labels)\n- Trial conditions: right/left correct hits, with or without autowater (WC context), excluding early-lick and stimulation trials\n\n---\n\n### `getSeq.m`\n**Path:** `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/getSeq.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction obj = getSeq(obj,params,prbnum,varargin)\n\nedges = params.tmin:params.dt:params.tmax;\nobj.time = edges + params.dt/2;\nobj.time = obj.time(1:end-1);\n\n% PSTH: [time × units × conditions]\nobj.psth{prbnum} = zeros(numel(obj.time), numel(params.cluid{prbnum}), numel(params.condition));\nfor i = 1:numel(params.cluid{prbnum})\n    for j = 1:numel(params.condition)\n        trix = params.trialid{j};\n        spkix = ismember(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trial, trix);\n        N = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm_aligned(spkix), edges);\n        N = N(1:end-1);\n        obj.psth{prbnum}(:,i,j) = mySmooth(N./numel(trix)./params.dt, params.smooth, params.bctype);\n    end\nend\n\n% Single trial data: [time × units × trials]\nobj.trialdat{prbnum} = zeros(numel(obj.time), numel(params.cluid{prbnum}), obj.bp.Ntrials);\nfor i = ...\n    for j = 1:obj.bp.Ntrials\n        N = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm_aligned(spkix), edges);\n        N = N(1:end-1);\n        obj.trialdat{prbnum}(:,i,j) = mySmooth(N./params.dt, params.smooth, params.bctype);\n    end\nend\nend\n```\n\n**Logic:** Bins spike times into `params.dt`-wide bins (5 ms), normalizes by number of trials and bin width to get firing rate in spikes/second. Applies `mySmooth` (15 ms causal Gaussian). Produces both trial-averaged PSTHs per condition and single-trial matrices.\n\n---\n\n### `findClusters.m`\n**Path:** `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/findClusters.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction idx = findClusters(qualityList, qualities)\nfor i = 1:numel(qualityList)\n    if ~ischar(qualityList{i}) && ~isstring(qualityList{i})\n        qualityList{i} = '';\n    end\nend\nqualityList = strtrim(qualityList);\n\nif any(strcmpi(qualities,'all')) || strcmpi(qualities{1},'all')\n    idx = find(~ismember(qualityList,'garbage') & ~ismember(qualityList,'gabrga') & ...\n               ~ismember(qualityList,'noisy') & ~ismember(qualityList,'real?'));\n    return\nend\n...\nend\n```\n\n**Logic:** With `quality='all'` (the default), returns all units except those labeled `'garbage'`, `'gabrga'` (typo variant), `'noisy'`, or `'real?'`. Otherwise uses pattern matching to find specified quality labels.\n\n---\n\n### `removeLowFRClusters.m`\n**Path:** `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/removeLowFRClusters.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction [obj,cluid] = removeLowFRClusters(obj,cluid,lowFR,prbnum)\nmeanFRs = mean(mean(obj.psth{prbnum},3,'omitnan'),'omitnan');\nuse = meanFRs > lowFR;\n\ncluid = cluid(use);\nobj.psth{prbnum} = obj.psth{prbnum}(:,use,:);\nobj.trialdat{prbnum} = obj.trialdat{prbnum}(:,use,:);\nend\n```\n\n**Logic:** Computes mean firing rate per unit by averaging the PSTH across all time points and conditions. Removes any unit with mean FR <= `lowFR` (default 0.5 Hz). Prunes both the `cluid` index array and the `psth` and `trialdat` arrays.\n\n---\n\n### `mySmooth.m`\n**Path:** `/app/code/utils/mySmooth.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction [out,kernsd] = mySmooth(x, N, varargin)\n% Causal Gaussian kernel smoothing, operates on first dimension (time)\n% N = window size in samples\n% varargin: 'reflect', 'zeropad', 'none' for boundary conditions\n\nif N == 0 || N == 1  % no smoothing\n    out = x; kernsd = 0; return\nend\n\nif isrow(x); x = x'; end\n\n% handle boundary condition\nif nargin > 2\n    bctype = varargin{1};\n    if strcmpi(bctype,'reflect')\n        x_filt = cat(1, x(1:N,:), x); trim = N + 1;\n    elseif strcmpi(bctype,'zeropad')\n        x_filt = cat(1, zeros(N,size(x,2)), x); trim = N + 1;\n    elseif strcmpi(bctype,'none')\n        x_filt = x; trim = 1;\n    end\nelse\n    x_filt = x; trim = 1;\nend\n\nkern = gausswin(N);\nkernsd = std(1:N);\nkern(1:floor(numel(kern)/2)) = 0;  % CAUSAL: zeros on the left half\nkern = kern ./ sum(kern);\n\nfor j = 1:Ncol\n    out(:,j) = conv(x_filt(:,j), kern, 'same');\nend\nout = out(trim:end,:);\nend\n```\n\n**Key logic:** A **causal** Gaussian kernel (left half zeroed out), applied via convolution. The `N=15` parameter means a 15-sample window; at 5 ms bins this is a 75 ms causal Gaussian. Supports boundary condition types: 'reflect', 'zeropad', or 'none' (default when called from processData).\n\n---\n\n### `findPosition.m`\n**Path:** `/app/code/funcs/kinematics/findPosition.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction [xpos, ypos] = findPosition(taxis, obj, nTrials, view, feat, alignEv)\n\ntraj = obj.traj{view};\nfeatix = findDLCFeatIndex(obj.traj, view, {feat});\n\nxpos = nan(numel(taxis), nTrials);\nypos = nan(numel(taxis), nTrials);\n\nvidshift = findVideoOffset(obj);\n\nfor i = 1:nTrials\n    % check for dropped frames\n    if isfield(traj(i),'NdroppedFrames')\n        if isnan(traj(i).NdroppedFrames); continue; end\n    end\n\n    % create frameTimes if missing (400 Hz assumed)\n    if ~isfield(traj(i),'frameTimes')\n        traj(i).frameTimes = (1:size(traj(i).ts,1)) ./ 400;\n    end\n\n    if ~isnan(traj(i).frameTimes)\n        ts = traj(i).ts(:,1:2,featix);\n        if ~contains(feat,'tongue')\n            ts = mySmooth(ts, 1, 'reflect');  % no smoothing (N=1) for non-tongue\n        end\n        % linear interpolation aligned to alignEv\n        tsinterp = interp1(traj(i).frameTimes - vidshift - obj.bp.ev.(alignEv)(i), ts, taxis);\n    end\n\n    xpos(:,i) = tsinterp(:,1);\n    ypos(:,i) = tsinterp(:,2);\n\n    if ~contains(feat,'tongue')\n        xpos(:,i) = fillmissing(xpos(:,i), 'nearest');\n        ypos(:,i) = fillmissing(ypos(:,i), 'nearest');\n    end\nend\nend\n```\n\n**Logic:** Extracts x/y position of a DLC-tracked feature for each trial. Interpolates from 400 Hz video frame times to the neural data time axis (`taxis`), correcting for video offset (`vidshift`) and aligning to the specified event. Tongue positions are left with NaNs for missing data; other features (jaw, paw, nose) have NaNs filled by nearest-value interpolation.\n\n---\n\n### `findVideoOffset.m`\n**Path:** `/app/code/funcs/findVideoOffset.m`\n\n```matlab\n% find offset between neural data file start and video file start\nfunction vidshift = findVideoOffset(obj)\nbitStart = mode(obj.bp.ev.bitStart);\nvidFileOffset = mode(obj.sglx.bitcode.bitstart) / obj.sglx.fs;\nvidshift = vidFileOffset - bitStart; % (s)\nend\n```\n\n**Logic:** Computes the temporal offset between the neural recording file start and the video file start, in seconds. `bitStart` is the behavioral event start time; `vidFileOffset` is computed from the SpikeGLX bitcode. The difference gives the video shift to apply when aligning video frame times to neural/behavioral time.\n\n---\n\n### `loadMotionEnergy.m`\n**Path:** `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/loadMotionEnergy.m`\n\n**Key logic:**\n1. Finds and loads `motionEnergy_[animal]_[date].mat` from `datapth/DataObjects/[animal]/`\n2. Computes `vidshift = findVideoOffset(obj)` for temporal alignment\n3. Interpolates ME data from 400 Hz frame times to the neural data time axis (`obj.time + params.advance_movement`) using `interp1`, aligned to `params.alignEvent` (goCue)\n4. Fallback: if `frameTimes` field is missing, constructs them as `(1:N) / 400` and uses a 0.5 s offset\n5. Fills NaN values using `fillmissing(...,'nearest')`\n6. Creates binary movement indicator: `me.move = me.data > me.moveThresh` (threshold stored per-session in the ME file)\n\n---\n\n### Trial Filtering Logic (`findTrials.m` + `processData.m`)\n\nThe default conditions from `getDefaultParams.m` are:\n- `R & hit & ~stim.enable & ~autowater & ~early` — right hits, DR task\n- `L & hit & ~stim.enable & ~autowater & ~early` — left hits, DR task\n- `R & hit & ~stim.enable & autowater & ~early` — right hits, WC task\n- `L & hit & ~stim.enable & autowater & ~early` — left hits, WC task\n\nThe `autowater` field distinguishes WC (autowater=1) from DR (autowater=0) context. The `~early` filter excludes early-lick trials. The `~stim.enable` filter excludes optogenetic stimulation trials. Miss/error trials and ignore trials are **commented out** in the default params.\n\n`findTrials.m` evaluates these as logical expressions over `obj.bp` fields, using `eval()`. It handles a `fidx` field (1 = early-lick pre-data, 2 = valid trials) via an offset to trial indices.\n\n---\n\n## 2. `methods.txt` Content\n\nThe agent read the full text of `/app/methods.txt` (59 lines). Key extracted information:\n\n**Paradigm:** Head-fixed mice performed two interleaved tasks:\n- **DR (Delayed-Response):** Auditory tone → delay epoch (0.9 s) → auditory go cue (chirp, 10 ms) → lick for reward\n- **WC (Water-Cued):** No auditory cues; water reward randomly delivered at one of two lickports\n\nBlocks of 10–25 trials alternated within session; sessions started with ~100 DR trials.\n\n**Trial filtering:** At least 40 correct DR trials per direction and 20 correct WC trials per direction. Early-lick and ignore trials excluded from all analyses.\n\n**Videography:** High-speed video at 400 Hz, two cameras (side + bottom). DeepLabCut tracking of tongue, jaw, nose, paws. Missing values filled with nearest available value (except tongue). Velocity = first-order derivative of position.\n\n**Motion energy:** Defined as absolute value of difference between median pixel values across next 5 frames (12.5 ms) and previous 5 frames (12.5 ms). Single value per frame = 99th percentile of ME across ~700 pixels. Threshold set per-session manually at the trough between the two modes of a bimodal ME distribution.\n\n**Spike sorting:** JRCLUST (v4.1.0) and/or Kilosort 3, with manual curation in Phy 2. Well-isolated single units: based on ISI histogram, separation from other units, stationarity across session. Multi-units: passed curation but higher ISI violation rate.\n\n**Session inclusion:** At least 10 units per session required.\n\n**Firing rate threshold:** Only units with FR > 1 Hz included in subspace alignment and single-unit selectivity analyses. **All units with FR > 1 Hz** included in all other analyses. (Note: the code uses 0.5 Hz as the default in `getDefaultParams.m`.)\n\n**Neural data:**\n- DR task only: 25 sessions, 9 mice, 1,651 units (483 single units)\n- Two-context (DR+WC): 12 sessions, 6 mice, 522 units (214 single units)\n- Randomized delay task: 19 sessions, 4 mice, 845 units (288 single units)\n\n**Choice selectivity:** 40 trials subsampled per direction; rank-sum test on spike counts per epoch (P < 0.05).\n\n**Context selectivity:** 40 trials each for DR and WC; rank-sum on spike counts during ITI (300 ms before sample tone, P < 0.05); additional check that preferred context is consistent across adjacent block pairs.\n\n---\n\n## 3. `paper.pdf`\n\nThe agent attempted to read `paper.pdf` at `/app/paper.pdf` but failed because `pdftoppm` (poppler-utils) was not installed. % 'jawOnset' 'goCue'  'moveOnset'  'firstLick'  'lastLick'\n\nparams.timeWarp            = 0;\nparams.nLicks              = 20;\n\nparams.lowFR               = 0.5; % remove clusters with firing rates less than this val\n\n% set conditions to calculate PSTHs for\nparams.condition(1)     = {'R&hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};   % right hits, no stim, aw off\nparams.condition(end+1) = {'L&hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};   % left hits, no stim, aw off\nparams.condition(end+1) = {'R&hit&~stim.enable&autowater&~early'};    % right hits, no stim, aw on\nparams.condition(end+1) = {'L&hit&~stim.enable&autowater&~early'};    % left hits, no stim, aw on\n\nparams.probe(1) = 1;\nparams.probeArea{1} = 'ALM';\n\nparams.tmin = -2.5;\nparams.tmax = 2.5;\nparams.dt = 1/200;   % 5 ms bins\n\nparams.smooth = 15;  % 15 ms Gaussian kernel\n\nparams.quality = {'all'};\n\nparams.traj_features = {{'tongue','left_tongue','right_tongue','jaw','trident','nose'},...\n                        {'top_tongue','topleft_tongue','bottom_tongue','bottomleft_tongue','jaw','top_paw','bottom_paw','top_nostril','bottom_nostril'}};\n\nparams.feat_varToExplain = 80;\n\nparams.prep = [-2.5 -0.05];  % prep epoch (s) relative to goCue\nparams.move = [-2.5 1.5];    % move epoch (s) relative to goCue\nparams.advance_movement = 0.0;\nend\n```\n\n**Key parameters:**\n- Alignment: `goCue`\n- Time window: -2.5 to +2.5 s, at 5 ms bins (dt = 1/200)\n- Firing rate threshold: 0.5 Hz\n- Smoothing: 15 ms causal Gaussian kernel\n- Quality filter: 'all' (excludes garbage/noisy labels)\n- Trial conditions: right/left correct hits, with or without autowater (WC context), excluding early-lick and stimulation trials\n\n---\n\n### `getSeq.m`\n**Path:** `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/getSeq.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction obj = getSeq(obj,params,prbnum,varargin)\n\nedges = params.tmin:params.dt:params.tmax;\nobj.time = edges + params.dt/2;\nobj.time = obj.time(1:end-1);\n\n% PSTH: [time × units × conditions]\nobj.psth{prbnum} = zeros(numel(obj.time), numel(params.cluid{prbnum}), numel(params.condition));\nfor i = 1:numel(params.cluid{prbnum})\n    for j = 1:numel(params.condition)\n        trix = params.trialid{j};\n        spkix = ismember(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trial, trix);\n        N = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm_aligned(spkix), edges);\n        N = N(1:end-1);\n        obj.psth{prbnum}(:,i,j) = mySmooth(N./numel(trix)./params.dt, params.smooth, params.bctype);\n    end\nend\n\n% Single trial data: [time × units × trials]\nobj.trialdat{prbnum} = zeros(numel(obj.time), numel(params.cluid{prbnum}), obj.bp.Ntrials);\nfor i = ...\n    for j = 1:obj.bp.Ntrials\n        N = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm_aligned(spkix), edges);\n        N = N(1:end-1);\n        obj.trialdat{prbnum}(:,i,j) = mySmooth(N./params.dt, params.smooth, params.bctype);\n    end\nend\nend\n```\n\n**Logic:** Bins spike times into `params.dt`-wide bins (5 ms), normalizes by number of trials and bin width to get firing rate in spikes/second. Applies `mySmooth` (15 ms causal Gaussian). Produces both trial-averaged PSTHs per condition and single-trial matrices.\n\n---\n\n### `findClusters.m`\n**Path:** `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/findClusters.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction idx = findClusters(qualityList, qualities)\nfor i = 1:numel(qualityList)\n    if ~ischar(qualityList{i}) && ~isstring(qualityList{i})\n        qualityList{i} = '';\n    end\nend\nqualityList = strtrim(qualityList);\n\nif any(strcmpi(qualities,'all')) || strcmpi(qualities{1},'all')\n    idx = find(~ismember(qualityList,'garbage') & ~ismember(qualityList,'gabrga') & ...\n               ~ismember(qualityList,'noisy') & ~ismember(qualityList,'real?'));\n    return\nend\n...\nend\n```\n\n**Logic:** With `quality='all'` (the default), returns all units except those labeled `'garbage'`, `'gabrga'` (typo variant), `'noisy'`, or `'real?'`. Otherwise uses pattern matching to find specified quality labels.\n\n---\n\n### `removeLowFRClusters.m`\n**Path:** `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/removeLowFRClusters.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction [obj,cluid] = removeLowFRClusters(obj,cluid,lowFR,prbnum)\nmeanFRs = mean(mean(obj.psth{prbnum},3,'omitnan'),'omitnan');\nuse = meanFRs > lowFR;\n\ncluid = cluid(use);\nobj.psth{prbnum} = obj.psth{prbnum}(:,use,:);\nobj.trialdat{prbnum} = obj.trialdat{prbnum}(:,use,:);\nend\n```\n\n**Logic:** Computes mean firing rate per unit by averaging the PSTH across all time points and conditions.
